The device joined, and signed a marker naming them. Once the store released the directory, the real key was made resident with no check against the chain held, the account's other devices' segments opened (on that chain the member is a writer, and the stream is the member's), and `resealSharedVault` sealed the real items again under the other party's key and published them: S6's breach. The marker dispute this left was noticed (`markerConflicts`) and ignored by every device holding a ledger, permanently, since no marker is retired. | **Fixed, in three parts.** (a) A key is used for a vault with a ledger only if that ledger hands it to this account: a granted key this device opened from a grant the ledger entitles the account to, or whose bytes such a grant shows the directory's copy to hold (`SharedVault.verifiedKeys`, saved signed as `WWVK` bound to the owner; `admitVaultKeys`, `withholdUnvouchedKeys` at unlock, when the directory adds keys and whenever ledgers load); the account's own genesis key only where the ledger's owner is this account; and a grant's key is refused where the store keeps other bytes under its ID. The review's rule, "a key ID some record on some branch names", was not enough: key IDs are public, and the other chain can name the real key's ID itself. (b) A vault whose owner the account's devices disagree about (two markers, or this device's ledger against its account's marker) is held closed on all of them, ledger or not (`isOwnerDisputed`, `SharedVaultError.ownerDisputed`): no current key, nothing sealed, sealed again or published, no proposal, plan, rotation, invitation or acceptance, not given to AutoFill, and listed as disputed (`VaultInfo.isOwnerDisputed`, `SharedVaultStatus.ownerDisputed`, `VaultRow.ownerDisputedReason`). (c) The coordinator's addition: `knownOwner` answers `nil` only once the session has listed the account's vault directory and found nothing of the vault there (`directoryVaults`). `AdversarialSharedVaultTests` "a member's new device led into another owner's chain never seals the account's items under its key", "a key the account's directory brings is not used under a ledger that never handed it to the account", "a chain that names the real key's ID does not get the real key used under it", "a device whose account's directory names a vault, or has not been listed yet, takes no stranger's word" (all four failed before: the real item sealed and published under the other chain's key, the real key resident, the invitation accepted), "a vault whose owner the account's devices disagree about is held closed on all of them, ledger or not". **Left, and the owner's to decide:** nothing ends a dispute. A marker is never retired, because the account key signs markers and a revoked device keeps it, so a retirement it could sign would reopen what the dispute closes; a retirement the person chooses on a device, signed by the account and a device registered now, with the joined chain then forgotten on the device that joined it (which S26 forbids today), is the design that would. Meanwhile the person reads the items and copies them into a vault of their own. A store that withholds the vault's whole directory still lets the device join the other chain; what the person writes there after accepting is the other party's to read, as with any invitation they accepted. A device of the account that has not yet seen either marker goes on until it does. |

The insider's own client there does not judge its own rows, as an adversary's need not. |

Either way the deletion gate opened: a member who moves an item between shared vaults and is made read-only in the second before syncing has the copy held aside, and the first vault's deletion then went out, erasing the original everywhere with its only copy in one device's quarantine. A8 (deleting or corrupting the index), or no adversary at all. | **Fixed.** The set is a row of its own (`OwnTakenOutRow`, `WWTO`, signed, saying whether it is complete), which a rebuild of the index leaves alone; the index is format 5 without it. A row that is missing or does not verify is not "none": until the quarantine holds no entry of this device's that the sync state does not know, every such entry holds deletions back, as before Z54, and then the set is known to be all there is. A quarantine that cannot be read holds deletions back and prunes nothing. `AdversarialSharedVaultTests` "a move's deletion still waits for its held-aside copy once the saved index, or the set itself, is gone" (both cases failed before: the original was erased); "Shared vaults: the stream index" "the rows beside the index…". **Not tested:** the failed read of the quarantine, which needs a state store that fails on demand. |

The test no longer rests on signing being deterministic: right after `shareVault`, with no sync, the owner's other device's head must already be the chain's, members and all, which failed before on every platform, and the listing comparison runs everywhere (`AdversarialSharedVaultTests` "the owner's other device takes up a vault its account shared by sharing it, until the vault has rotated"). |

A row counts if the item's clock holds an edit of this device's above every version of it the device published (one that does not open counts). "Shared vaults through VaultSession" "changes nobody else has are this device's unpublished edits, not the merges it sealed" (failed before on the merge; holds the unpublished edit of an existing item). |

What this part of the audit did not settle. **Decisions that are the owner's.** Whether a vault whose log was
compacted may be shared, and by which attribution rule (Z42; refused meanwhile); whether every account granted the
current key by a record that lost a fork should force a rotation, or only one its branch went on to remove (Z43);
whether the ledger and pins are to be synced between an account's devices, so that a second device need not accept
the invitation again (S35, Z41); a leave operation on the transport's share (Z45); and whether, and how, the person
may end a dispute between the account's devices about a vault's owner, which today holds the vault closed for good
(Z59). **Engine ground.** Since Z157 no foreign segment verifies in a device's stream; what a stream does at an
object that never verifies at all is the engine's own finding (Z24), and a snapshot that attributes versions to a device it was not written
by is judged entry by entry here and no further. **The Mac.** Everything here was built and run on Linux against
swift-crypto. `SQLiteSharingStore` and `SQLiteRecordStore` have not run with the new marker rows or the second wrap
class, nor `SQLiteSharingStore` with the two rows Z59 and Z121 add (`vaultKeys`, `ownTakenOut`: new values of its text
key, no change of schema), the three files under `Apps/Shared` that changed have not been compiled, and no UI test has driven a shared
vault from the same account's other device since that device began holding it closed.

### AutoFill, matching and what an import can do to them (Z60 to Z79)

The adversaries are those of architecture §2: a current member of a shared vault who may write (**A4**), a page on a
look-alike host or a relying party (**A6**), and whoever hands the person an import file (**A7**). Z60, Z61 and the
first part of Z62 were fixed first; the tests named in their rows were written with the fix, and the record of that
work does not say they were run against the code as audited. Every later row's reproduction was run against the
unfixed code and failed for the reason the row gives, except Z67 and Z68, whose tests name screen-model properties
that did not exist before. *Mac only* marks what changed under `Apps/` and has not been compiled. Z70 to Z79 were
found by an independent review of the Z60 to Z69 fixes (its eleven points; the last three share Z79), and each was
reproduced by a test run against the code before its fix, or with the fix's check removed where the test needs an
API the fix adds; the rows say which.

| ID | Severity | Finding and threat | Disposition |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Z60 | High | AutoFill ranked every vault's items as one pool and no entry, candidate or proposal named its vault, so a member of a shared vault (A4) could plant an ordinary, policy-acceptable item that outranked or tied with the victim's own login and became the destination of writes a page started. With the victim's login saved by the extension (exact host `www.shop.example`), an item scoped `shop.example` with the victim's user name was the only match for relying party `shop.example`, and a conditional registration put the victim's new passkey into it with no UI, sealed under the key A4 holds. An item scoped more specifically hid the victim's login and was the only "update" offered for the next password; one tied with it could copy its title, user name and host so that the two rows were identical. The same pooling let a planted item withdraw the victim's QuickType identities and made a look-alike host "recognised" by a member's scope. The sharing audit's second finding is this one seen from the vault's side. | **Fixed.** A vault is a trust domain in the AutoFill core: `CredentialVault.credentialSnapshot()` says which vaults other accounts write (the session's `isMarkedShared`; a vault it does not describe counts as shared). An item of a shared vault never shadows one of another vault (`ItemRanker`; where the two classes meet the answer is `chooseAmong`, never a suggestion or a silent fill); its scope vouches for no look-alike of another vault's site (`FillRefusal.resemblesSavedSite`); nothing writes to it without UI (the conditional passkey upgrade, silent saves); it is never the pre-selected destination of a passkey; a new item never copies its scope; every entry, candidate and proposal carries its `EntryVault`, and a sheet says when everyone in the vault will see the secret. QuickType identities stay withdrawn, never redirected. Items of a vault the account is no longer a member of were already withheld from `credentialItems()` (Z45). Architecture §9.2. "Adversarial: AutoFill request model" (the vault-trust tests), "Adversarial: shared vaults through the session" (a member's planted login never receives another member's passkey; a member's more specific item neither hides another member's login nor takes its next password), "Adversarial: AutoFill service matching", "Adversarial: phishing resistance", `ItemRankerTests`. |
| Z61 | Medium | The item editor added any host the scope policy accepted as a scope, with subdomains, no assessment and no confirmation, and the detail view then showed a Cyrillic look-alike as the name it imitates. `05-anti-phishing.md` §3 described the confirmation naming the site as the sole route to using a suspicious host, in the present tense; only the import's merge screen had one. With a bare look-alike host typed or pasted into Websites (a full URL is refused by the normaliser), AutoFill filled the real item on the phishing page and every host below it. | **Fixed.** In the session, not the view: `VaultSession.save(_:confirmingHosts:)` judges every host a save adds against `SavedSites` (what the gate ranks among, every vault) and refuses one that resembles a saved site or cannot be read by eye with `VaultError.hostsNeedConfirmation` unless the call names it. `ItemEditorModel` turns the refusal into one question per host naming the site as it resolves, its reach and the item; the editor's dialog and the detail view's ASCII host and reach are *Mac only*. `05-anti-phishing.md` §3, `03-implementation-plan.md` and `04-test-plan.md` §6.7 now say what is built. "Adversarial: account screens" (adding a look-alike host by hand needs a confirmation that names the site and the item). |
| Z62 | High | A new import entry could take over AutoFill for a saved site. An entry becomes one item scoped to every URL it lists, and was imported with no question unless it duplicated an item on one host and login name. Scoped one label below a saved login (`login.bank.example` beside `bank.example`), it outranked it: the extension stopped offering the real login on the sign-in page, filled the file's password, and offered the imported item for update when the person typed the real one, which was then offered on the file's other host. An entry the file marks archived hid the saved login while offering nothing. Duplicates were looked for in the target vault only, so an entry tying with a login in another vault raised nothing either. The preview showed the hosts raw on one caption line. | **Fixed.** The preview judges every entry against `SavedSites`, every vault, and reports an `ImportConcern` for one that would hide a saved account on a host it is offered on, stand beside one, or name a host that resembles a saved site or cannot be read; an archived or (from an archive) excluded entry is judged like any other and says so. `commitImport` refuses without a decision for each (`decisionRequired`). The comparison runs off the actor (`AwayWork.importReview`) under a budget, past which hosts are reported unchecked and need the decision too; an entry's own other hosts vouch for nothing. The screen lists each concern with the accounts it would hide, named with their vaults, and shows every entry's hosts through `MergeHost` (the view is *Mac only*). Not done, and tracked: the save plan does not yet list every host an update candidate is offered on (the suggestion's third part). Architecture §14. "Adversarial: interchange through the session" (an entry that would outrank a saved login, an archived one, one beside another vault's login, a look-alike entry, the budget, a lock during the review), `InterchangeScreenTests`. |
| Z63 | Medium | The scope policy refused any host on the embedded suffix list whatever its reach, although an exact-host scope spans no other owner. Several listed suffixes are their operator's own sign-in site (`wordpress.com`, `substack.com`, `tumblr.com`, `bandcamp.com`): a login there could not be saved from the page, typed in the editor or imported, no passkey could be registered or asserted, a synced scope for one never ranked, and with nothing saved no look-alike of them was warned about, leaving the person copying passwords by hand. The other half of the finding, a tenant-hosting parent the list omits being scoped to all its tenants, is the known tail of the small list (D1, K6, `07-supply-chain.md` §6). | **Fixed** (the first part). `PublicSuffix.signInAtSuffix` (the shared-hosting entries the repository's site directory lists, and `bandcamp.com`) is accepted as an exact-host scope and refused with subdomains; the rest of the list (`github.io`, `herokuapp.com`, the country-code second-level domains) stays refused either way. `ScopePolicy.newScope(forHost:)` gives such a host that reach in the editor, an imported URL and `RelyingPartyID.scope`; the `www` apex is never a suffix. **Tracked** (the second part): no entry was added to the list; the audit's examples (`pythonanywhere.com`, `neocities.org`, `altervista.org`) wait for the full-PSL decision or an entry-by-entry review. `ScopePolicySuffixTests`, `SaveAndGenerateRequestTests`, `PasskeyAuthenticatorTests`, `ImportMappingTests`. |
| Z64 | Low | `HostNormalizer`'s IPv6 branch returned any run of hex digits, colons and dots before the length, structural-character, forbidden-scalar and label checks. `a::b.cafe.de` normalised and was a subdomain of a saved `cafe.de` scope, normalisation inventing a match; a megabyte of `a:` escaped C5's bound; and, because `Character.isHexDigit` accepts fullwidth digits, a colon-bearing host could hold characters no other path admits, unmapped. No browser or OS path is known to hand the extension such a domain identifier. | **Fixed.** The literal is parsed on ASCII bytes as RFC 4291 writes an address (groups of one to four hex digits, at most one `::`, an optional dotted-quad tail), bounded at 45 bytes before anything else, and `ScopeMatcher` never treats a colon-bearing host as below a scope. C5's bound now has no exception. "Adversarial: AutoFill service matching" (IPv6 literals), `HostNormalizerTests`. |
| Z65 | Low | The import's merge screen judged look-alikes against the stored items as they were: trashed items, and scopes such as `com` that the store keeps when another member or device wrote them, made a look-alike host "recognised", so it carried no warning and no second confirmation. The verifier added the easier route: a member (A4) planting an ordinary item scoped exactly to the look-alike survives any filter and recognises the host for every vault. | **Fixed** with Z61: `SavedSites` is the one view the gate, the editor and the import screens judge against (trashed items out, only acceptable scopes, and a shared vault's scope vouching for its own vault only), so neither route recognises the host for the person's own item. `MergeHostTests` (a policy-refused or trashed scope; a member's item scoped to the look-alike). |
| Z66 | Low | A relying party (A6) could fill a passkey's item until it no longer saved. Each largeBlob write is bounded at 16 KiB but was not bounded in number; each replaced the passkey field, and the vault keeps every earlier field set in the item's history, which is never pruned. About forty-five sign-ins with fresh blobs took the item past its 1 MiB payload, after which every edit of it failed: a password change, a report flag, an edit in the app. Deletion still worked, so it was a denial of service on one item. | **Fixed.** A field takes one write per `largeBlobInterval` (a day, as reports), and none that would take the item past `largeBlobItemBudget` (half the payload limit); a refused write is reported as not written and the assertion stands. The rejected alternative, keeping replaced blobs out of history, would break F1.4. Architecture §9.1. "Adversarial: AutoFill request model" (largeBlob writes cannot fill an item until it no longer saves), `PasskeyRequestTests`. |
| Z67 | Low | A merge adds an entry's other fields (text, phone number, security question, custom) and notes with no per-field choice, by design, but the duplicate screen showed only their labels and the word "notes". A7's entry for the bank, matching on one host and user name, could carry a "Sign-in page" text field holding the attacker's URL and a note saying the bank moved its login; the person who chose Merge never saw the values that landed in the trusted item. | **Fixed** on the screen, where the verifier put the fault: `MergeReview.otherFieldValues` and `notes` carry the values, and the screen shows them in full before the merge is chosen (secrets behind Show). `merged` is unchanged; the view is *Mac only*. `InterchangeScreenTests` (a merge's other fields and notes are shown with their values). |
| Z68 | Low | The import screens passed none of `ImportOptions`: the `www` apex widening documented as opt-out (K6, architecture §14) could be declined only by cancelling the import, and `includesTrashed` was unreachable. | **Fixed.** `ImportModel.addsApexOfWWWHosts` and `includesTrash`, chosen before the file is read, and the review states which reading of the addresses it shows; the toggles are *Mac only*. An archive's scopes are not widened, so the line is absent for a restore. `InterchangeScreenTests` (the apex widening can be turned off). |
| Z69 | Low | `05-anti-phishing.md` described two controls in the present tense that did not exist: the confirmation naming the site when a host is added in the app (§3), and an assessment banner when a secret is revealed on an item whose scopes the person is searching away from (L6), while the implementation plan and the test plan listed both as still to come. | **Docs corrected.** The first is now built (Z61) and the document says how. **Tracked:** the reveal banner is not built; L6 says so, and nothing claims it. |
| Z70 | Medium | Found by the review of this change (Z60's "never a suggestion"). The planner withheld a password or code identity only where another item at the host shared its name, comparing exact strings and ignoring the vault. The victim's own login covers `shop.example`, user `alice@x`; a member (A4) saves an item in the shared vault scoped to the whole site with user `аlice@x` (Cyrillic а) or `alice@x ` (trailing space). The names differ by bytes, so both identities were published, and the shared one passed the gate because the victim's own item makes the host recognised. QuickType showed two rows that look alike and name no vault; a tap on A4's released A4's password with no UI, and the victim signed in to an account A4 controls. The same for one-time-code identities, and for passkeys through the system's sheet, where the gate answers `chooseAmong` with both. | **Fixed** as the review proposed. Where an item of the other trust class (`VaultSnapshot.crossesTrust`: different vaults, one of them shared) is scoped to the host, neither has a password or code identity for that site, whatever the names; a shared vault's passkey is not published where the gate offers an item of another vault beside it for the relying party (`meetsAnotherTrustClass`), and a stale identity of one handed back gets `requiresInteraction(.ambiguousMatch)`, as a stale password identity does. The extension's list, which names the vault, is unchanged. Architecture §9.1 (rule 3), §9.2. "Adversarial: AutoFill request model" ("a member cannot steer a keyboard suggestion with a look-alike user name at the person's own site", "a member's passkey is not suggested where the person's own account for the relying party is"; both failed before: both identities published, the stale ones answered). |
| Z71 | Medium | Found by the review of this change (Z66's "bounded in effect"). Only largeBlob writes asked the item budget. Every field change, a meta-only one included, adds a history entry holding all the item's fields, so a relying party (A6) that had written one 16 KiB blob, as the budget allows, then sent one `passkeyRenamed` report a day with alternating names (or toggled the unknown flag with `acceptedPasskeys`) added about 22 KB a day, each copying the blob; a few weeks after the item reached the budget it passed the 1 MiB payload limit, and every edit of it failed, a password change included. | **Fixed.** Every write the model makes for a remote party asks `admitsRemoteWrite` (the item as written plus one history entry holding its fields) against `remoteWriteItemBudget`, half the payload limit (`largeBlobItemBudget` is the same constant): a report that would pass it is not applied and is counted in `ReportResult.overBudget`; a conditional passkey upgrade past it is `noItemToUpgrade`, and the site may ask again with UI. The person's own writes are not bounded by it. Not recording history for meta-only changes was not done: F1.4 keeps every version. Architecture §9.1, §9.2. "Adversarial: AutoFill request model" ("a relying party's reports cannot fill an item until it no longer saves", failed before: `payloadTooLarge` on the person's edit after two months of reports); `PasskeyRequestTests` ("a conditional request does not upgrade an item it would take past the remote-write budget", failed with the check removed). |
| Z72 | Medium | Found by the review of this change (latent; Z61 and Z62 placed enforcement in the session). `transfer`, behind `copy`, `move` and the move that restores a deleted vault's leftovers, checked only `ScopePolicy`. A member (A4) plants "Bank" in the shared vault, scoped to `login.bank.example` exactly and to `bаnk.example`, with the victim's user name; there it shadows nothing of the victim's and vouches for nothing (Z60). Copied or moved to Personal, it outranked the victim's own Bank login on the sign-in page, so the next password typed there was offered only to it, and its look-alike, now recognised by an own scope, filled that password on `bаnk.example`. No screen calls `copy` or `move` yet. | **Fixed** in `transfer`, as the review proposed. A copy or move whose source vault is shared (or undescribed) is judged against `SavedSites` without the item itself, as an import entry is: `transferConcern(itemID:to:)` gives the accounts it would hide or stand beside (`overlap`) and its hosts that resemble a saved site or cannot be read (`standing`, the item's other scopes compared for resemblance only, Z74), and `copy`/`move` refuse with `VaultError.transferNeedsConfirmation` unless `confirming` is that concern, before anything is written. Between the person's own vaults nothing is asked. Architecture §7.7. "Adversarial: shared vaults through the session" ("a copy or move out of a shared vault does not bring a member's scopes in unasked"; failed with the check removed, which is the code as it was: the copy went through). **Tracked:** no screen offers copy or move yet, so no view words the concern. |
| Z73 | Medium-low | Found by the review of this change (Z62's "any entry"). The review skipped an entry from the source's trash, and the commit created it live and moved it to the trash in a second write. With "include trash" on, an A7 file of trashed entries scoped `login.bank.example` raised no concern; a `lock()` between the two writes (an auto-lock during a long import) or any `VaultError` from the trash left the just-created item live and unreviewed, hiding the saved bank login on its sign-in page; and a later Restore from the trash did the same with no warning. | **Fixed** as the review proposed. `createImported` takes `trashed` and sets `trashedAt` in the one write that creates the item; trashed entries are reviewed like any other (`ImportConcern.isTrashed`, worded "it goes into the trash here, and would do this once restored"), so the decision is made before the item can ever rank. Architecture §14. "Adversarial: interchange through the session" ("a trashed entry is reviewed, and saved in the trash in one write"; failed before: no concern, and the item's version two above the saved one, a second write; the version check also failed with only the old two writes restored). |
| Z74 | Medium-low | Found by the review of this change (Z62). The intra-entry comparison had been removed as quadratic, so an entry's hosts were compared only with what is saved. An A7 entry scoped {`amazon.com`, `amaz0n.com`} going into a vault that holds no Amazon item raised nothing; the imported item filled on `amaz0n.com`, and so did every later password the person saved to it from `amazon.com`. The quadratic rationale does not hold: an entry has at most `maximumURLsPerItem` (32) URLs. | **Fixed.** `SavedSites.Prepared.standing(ofNewHost:resemblingOnly:)` compares a host with the entry's other scopes for resemblance only (the `others.assess` half; a host they cover is not thereby recognised), and `importConcerns` uses it, charging each host its comparisons with the saved scopes and with the entry's other hosts to the preview's budget: at most 32 × 31 an entry, linear in the file. `transfer` (Z72) uses the same comparison for a member's item. Consequence: an honest entry whose hosts differ by a character or two in their names (`shop1.example`, `shop2.example`) now needs a decision too. Architecture §14. "Adversarial: interchange through the session" ("a look-alike beside its original in one entry needs a decision"; failed before: no concern); `reviewBudget` updated for the new charge. |
| Z75 | Low-medium | Found by the review of this change. The merge path had neither the overlap check nor a session-level look-alike check: `commitImport` accepted any `MergeChoices.hosts`, and the look-alike confirmation lived only in `ImportModel`/`MergeReview`. An A7 entry duplicates the victim's Shop login (`shop.example`, `wendy`) and adds `login.bank.example`, shown as ordinary because it sits under a saved `bank.example`. Accepted on one switch, the Shop item outranked the Bank login there: the shop password was offered on the bank page, the Bank login hidden, and the next bank password updated the Shop item. Choosing merge also cleared the entry's concern requirement. | **Fixed** in the session. `commitImport` judges every host a merge adds that the item does not hold (`checkMergedHosts`, off the actor, with the existing item left out of what it would affect): `overlap` against `SavedSites` and `standing` beside the item's own scopes, and refuses the first merge whose unconfirmed host needs it with `InterchangeSessionError.mergeNeedsConfirmation(importedID:concern:)`, before anything is written and with the preview kept. `MergeChoices.confirmedHosts` names the hosts the person accepted a second time; the merge screen's `MergeHost` now warns for a host that would hide or stand beside another account (naming them) as for a look-alike, and `confirmAwaitingHost` records the confirmation. Architecture §14, `05-anti-phishing.md`. "Adversarial: interchange through the session" ("a merge that adds a host taking over another saved login, or a look-alike, needs the host confirmed"; failed with the check removed, which is the code as it was: the merge went through); "Adversarial: account screens" ("a merge host that would hide another saved login is named and held for a second confirmation"). The view's dialog is generic and unchanged. |
| Z76 | Low | Found by the review of this change. A host a shared vault's scope matched was compared only with what lay outside the vaults that recognise it, for a host being added to an item as for a page, and recognition from any vault counted. A member (A4) puts `аmazon.com` into the household vault, where the only Amazon login is; the victim adds `аmazon.com` to an item of their own (or to the household's Amazon item) and is asked nothing, because the host reads as recognised; that item then fills on the look-alike. | **Fixed for a host being added; the page half not changed.** `PhishingDetector.SiteIndex.assess(_:forItemIn:)`, behind `SavedSites.Prepared.standing(…, inVault:)`, lets only the person's own vaults and the item's own vault recognise the host, and where only shared vaults match compares it with every saved scope that does not itself match it, the matching vault's included. The editor (`hostsNeedingConfirmation`), the import review, the merge check (Z75), the transfer check (Z72) and the merge screen's `MergeHost` all pass the item's vault; `nil` lets no shared vault vouch. The review's first suggestion for the gate (compare a page's host with the recognising vault's other scopes too) was not taken: within one shared vault a member who can add the look-alike could as well edit the item it resembles, which is the decision `AdversarialPhishingTests` holds, and a host that mixes scripts is refused there whatever. Architecture §9. "Adversarial: AutoFill request model" ("a member's look-alike in a shared vault vouches for no item the person adds it to"; failed with the old comparison restored). |
| Z77 | Low | Found by the review of this change (a residual of Z41). On the owner's devices the owner's key did not arrive by grant, so a vault counted as shared only by the account's marker row and the sharing state, both in the App Group store. A8 deletes both while the app is locked; the AutoFill extension never syncs them back, so `isMarkedShared` was false there, and items a member (A4) had planted earlier shadowed the owner's logins and took conditional passkey upgrades with no UI. | **Fixed, for what AutoFill and the save checks are given.** `credentialSnapshot()` and `savedSites()` describe as shared every vault other than Personal that holds an item whose clock names a device outside this account (`vaultsWrittenOutsideTheAccount`: the registry's devices, revoked ones included, and only this device when no registry can be read). The clock is inside the ciphertext, authenticated under the vault key, so deleting a row cannot remove it. The review's suggestion, the row's `deviceID`, was not used: it is a plain column the same A8 can rewrite. **Left:** a vault in which no other account has written yet reads as the owner's own after such a deletion, which is harmless to AutoFill (nothing of a member's is there); `isMarkedShared` itself, which the write paths ask, is unchanged. "Adversarial: shared vaults through the session" ("a vault whose items another account wrote is shared on the owner's device after its marker is deleted"; failed before: described as the owner's alone, and the member's login took the passkey). |
| Z78 | Low | Found by the review of this change. Fail-open entry points stayed public: `VaultSession.credentialItems()`, `CredentialIdentityPlanner.identities(for: [Item])` and `difference(from:to:)` over items, and the `[Item]` overloads of `publishAll` and `publishChange` take every vault as the person's own, so the natural call publishes a shared vault's items as own; and `saveCredentialItem` saved with `.confirmedByCaller`, a public bypass of Z61's look-alike check for any caller of the session's `CredentialVault` conformance (a new item scoped `amaz0n.com` beside a saved `amazon.com` went in unasked). | **Fixed.** The four `[Item]` entry points and `credentialItems()` are internal (tests reach them through `@testable`). `CredentialVault.saveCredentialItem` takes `confirmingHosts` (the two-argument form, a protocol extension, names none), `VaultSession` judges every added host as `save` does, `.confirmedByCaller` is gone, and the model names a host only where its save or registration sheet showed `NewItemProposal.warning` and the person chose the new item; a refusal is `CredentialVaultError.hostsNeedConfirmation`. Architecture §9.2. "Adversarial: AutoFill request model" ("the session's credential-vault save cannot add a look-alike host unnamed"; failed before: the item was saved; also shows the model's warned save going through over a real session). |
| Z79 | Low | Found by the review of this change: its last three points. (a) Z63's side effect: `PhishingDetector`'s "same name under another suffix" allowance returned before the out-of-position check, so a tenant named after its hosting operator (`bandcamp.bandcamp.com`) beside a saved `bandcamp.com` read as unrecognised: nothing filled, and nothing warned. (b) The import review's `overlap()` ran outside `maximumImportHostComparisons`: linear in the file, but a member (A4) can put any number of items on one host, and every entry ranks them. (c) Z60's "nothing writes to it without UI" did not cover a relying party's reports and largeBlob writes, which still change a shared vault's items silently. | **Fixed** (a, b) **and docs corrected** (c). (a) The allowance does not apply to a host below the saved host itself. (b) `SavedSites.Prepared.overlap` takes a `limit` and counts `comparisons` (two rankings of the relevant items per host), `importConcerns` charges it to the preview's budget, and an entry past it is `overlapIsTruncated`, which needs the decision. (c) CLAUDE.md now says reports and largeBlob writes still reach a shared vault's item without extension UI, carrying no secret of the person's and bounded by `remoteWriteItemBudget` (Z71), as architecture §9.2 already did. "Adversarial: AutoFill request model" ("a tenant named after its hosting operator is a look-alike of the operator's saved sign-in site"; failed before); "Adversarial: interchange through the session" ("the review's overlap is charged to its budget, and an entry past it needs a decision"; failed with the charge removed). |

What this part of the audit did not settle. **Decisions that are the owner's.** Which further hosting suffixes are
their operator's sign-in site (`PublicSuffix.signInAtSuffix` is a judgement per operator), and whether to adopt the
full Public Suffix List for the tenant parents it omits (Z63); whether the reveal banner is wanted (Z69); whether an
update offered from the save sheet should list every host the item is offered on (Z62). **The Mac.** Everything here
was built and run on Linux. `Apps/Shared/ImportView.swift`, `AccessibilityID.swift`, `ItemEditView.swift`,
`ItemDetailView.swift` and `AppModel.swift` changed and have not been compiled; no UI test drives the editor's
confirmation, the import's concern section or its options. Rows Z70 to Z79 changed nothing under `Apps/`; the merge
screen's new warning (Z75) reaches the existing confirmation dialog, which has not run with it. **From the review,
the owner's:** whether the gate should also judge a page's host against the other scopes of the one shared vault
that recognises it (Z76; not done, by the earlier decision that a member could as well edit the item), whether
`isMarkedShared` itself, which the write paths ask, should count an item written from outside the account (Z77;
AutoFill and the save checks do), and how a screen should word a copy's or move's concern once one offers them
(Z72).

### Files on disk, exports, the Emergency Kit, hygiene's cache and the app shells (Z80 to Z99)

The adversaries are those of architecture §2: **A1** the cloud drive the person may save a file into, **A2** and
**A3** with the device, **A4** a writing member of a shared vault, **A7** whoever hands the person an import file or
an archive with its Kit, **A8** whoever reads the app's files or memory on a Mac after it locks, and U1's adversary,
who can present the enrolled face or reaches the unlocked app but does not know the account password. The tests
named were written with each fix, on Linux; none was run against the code as audited, because most of these
findings are an absence (no sweep, no budget, no password, no partition) that a test can only name through the API
that fills it. Where a test still shows the old behaviour, the row says so. *Mac only* marks what changed under
`Apps/` or in a guarded `WatchwordPlatform` file and has not been compiled; Z91 to Z94 are unused. Z99 and Z126
to Z134 were found by an independent review of the Z80 to Z98 fixes (its ten points, in its order); this block was
full at Z99, so they continue at Z126, after the last row of this section (the sharing rows end at Z125).

| ID | Severity | Finding and threat | Disposition |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Z80 | Medium | A staged plaintext export survived the process. "Save File…" wrote the whole export (every password, for CSV and 1PUX) to `tmp/Export-<UUID>/`, and every removal (the mover's completion, a cancel, the screen closing, a lock) read the export model's memory, so a crash, force quit, jetsam kill or power loss with the mover up left the file, and no later launch looked for it: on macOS with no protection class, readable by A8 after the vault locked; on iOS with `completeUnlessOpen`, for a forensic reader. The verifier narrowed the iOS route (backgrounding locks at once by default, and the lock removed the copy) and the macOS quit route (unverified); crash, force quit and shutdown need neither. | **Fixed.** One fixed, backup-excluded folder (`StagingFolder`, `<tmp>/Watchword Staging/`, a sub-folder per file), swept whole, independently of any model's state, at launch before the session is built (`AppEnvironment.make`), at every unlock (`AppModel.enterVault`), at every lock (`StagingSweeper` on the lock signal) and at termination; the sweep also removes the earlier `Export-*` folders. The decision and the sweep are portable; the hooks are *Mac only*. What a dead process leaves lasts until the next launch (§14.1 says so). "Screen models: staged files". |
| Z81 | Medium | No import-wide budget on stored size. The vault stores every line of notes again with its sync metadata (`StoredItemSize` budgets 272 bytes a line), and only fields, URLs, tags and history counted against an import-wide budget, so A7's CSV rows of a title and 2,700 line feeds (under 3 KB each, inside every per-entry limit) stored as about 730 KiB each: a few megabytes of CSV, or a 1PUX a hundredth of that compressed, committed hundreds of megabytes of sealed rows, published to every device of the account and decrypted by every listing and AutoFill request. | **Fixed.** `InterchangeLimits.maximumTotalStoredBytes`, 512 MiB, the sum of the drafts' estimates, counted in `ImportAssembler.add` and `addArchived` as each draft is kept; reaching it fails the file. It is above what the million-field budget's small fields store, so it binds only on shapes that store far more than they count. The fuzz harness's `check` asserts the sum. "Adversarial: import parsers" ("A file whose small rows store as large items fails on the import-wide stored-size budget", which also shows the amplification, about 270 times a row). |
| Z82 | Medium | An archive the app wrote could be unrestorable. Restore applied another app's file limits (`InterchangeLimits.standard`) to the archive: more than 50,000 items failed the whole file though the format holds 200,000 (X3 had been fixed so such an archive could be restored), an item the vault stores between about 0.75 and 1 MiB was skipped, and nothing at export time checked or said so ("Everything is in the backup"). The verifier corrected the A4 route: a skipped item adds nothing to the field budget, so the whole file fails only through items each under the stored-size bound, about 150 of them; it still works. The restore screen's footer also said every item comes back, while the person's trash did not. | **Fixed.** `InterchangeLimits.archive`, used only by `ArchivedItems`: the format's 200,000 entries, the vault's 1 MiB payload, 16,384 parts an item, one field for every 64 bytes of the largest archive (held to `ItemCodec` by a test), 1 GiB stored. `exportArchive` runs the items through the restore's own assembler off the actor (`ArchivedItems.restoreCheck`, `AwayWork.archiveRestoreCheck`) and returns what would not come back; the export screen lists it. The restore screen offers the trash and its footer says so (*Mac only*). "Items from an encrypted Watchword archive" (50,001 items; an item past the import bound; the restore check), "the archive's limits are the vault's and the format's own", "Adversarial: interchange through the session" (the check's step locks like the others). |
| Z83 | Low | The export's "zeroised" bytes were wiped in a copy. `ExportModel` did `var bytes = export.bytes; SecureBytes(wiping: &bytes)` while `export` still held the array, so the wipe copied the buffer first and zeroed the copy, and the session's buffer, the whole file, was freed unwiped; staging then made `Data(contents.unprotectedCopy())`. §14.1 and Q5 said the bytes stayed in zeroising memory. | **Fixed, and the claim narrowed.** `PlaintextExport.bytes` is `SecureBytes`: the session moves the exporter's array out of its result and wipes it while it is the buffer's only reference; staging writes one `Data` made from protected storage and zeroes it. What the exporters build on the way (CSV or JSON text, the ZIP's parts) is ordinary memory, like every item's `String`s (D5); §14.1 says so. "Secure bytes" ("wiping reaches a buffer only through its sole reference", which passes on the old code too: it is the mechanism). |
| Z84 | Low | Q5's sibling: the Emergency Kit PDF, the Secret Key in text and QR form, still went through `fileExporter` with a `FileDocument`, with no protection class and no clean-up; the PDF sat in a view's `@State` as plain `Data` until the exporter's completion (the network verifier's addition); `EmergencyKitDocument`'s comment still said the key never lands in a temporary directory, which G's C2 recorded as corrected; and nothing told the person not to save the kit into the synced drive, where it would sit beside the ciphertext and archives A1 already has, leaving the password as the only barrier (ADR 0008). | **Fixed.** `EmergencyKitModel` writes the PDF (`EmergencyKitDocument.write`, which zeroes its rendered buffer) into the staging folder with `.completeFileProtection` on iOS and hands it to the file mover; the copy goes after the mover, on close and on lock, and with Z80's sweep; no view holds the PDF. The comment says what is true, and the kit and the screen carry `EmergencyKitText.storageWarning`. Whether the system staged anything for `fileExporter` was never settled and no longer matters. The view and the PDF are *Mac only*. "Screen models: Emergency Kit and revealed secrets"; "Emergency Kit PDF" (*Mac only*). |
| Z85 | Low | File-chosen dates were kept without the clock check `ImportedEntry`'s contract required, so A7's item dated in the year 9000 pinned `modifiedAt` on every device for good (sync keeps it monotonic), kept the "old password" check from firing and dated its trash; and an archive's `Field.meta` came back verbatim, so a crafted `autofill.lastReport` far in the future rate-limited that field's relying-party reports for ever (the archive route needs its Kit, as the verifier noted). | **Fixed.** The session bounds every kept date by its clock before a preview (`ImportResult.boundingDates(notAfter:)`, reported as `futureDateBounded`), for every format and the archive; `addArchived` drops the AutoFill extension's `autofill.*` keys, current and in history, reported as `autoFillStateNotRestored`. "Adversarial: interchange through the session" ("a file cannot date an item in the future or restore the AutoFill extension's state"). |
| Z86 | Low | `emergencyKit()` handed the Secret Key to any unlocked session, quick-unlocked included, while export, archive, enrolment of quick unlock and revocation all re-derive the password. U1's adversary opens Settings, shows the kit and photographs the QR; with the sync store's ciphertext (A1) the password is then the only barrier, permanently, until F10.1's re-key exists. The old behaviour was what "the Emergency Kit can be shown again while unlocked" asserted. | **Fixed.** `emergencyKit(password:)` re-derives the password under the stored header (`verifyAccountPassword`); `unconfirmedEmergencyKit()` shows the unconfirmed kit again without it only while the account records no confirmation and the keys were opened with the password in this lock epoch, so a quick unlock, or a confirmation column cleared by A8, gets `accountPasswordRequired`. Settings asks for the password on the kit's own screen (`EmergencyKitModel`; `kit.password`, `kit.open`; *Mac only*), and the UI-test peer's hook opens it with the launch password. No UI test opened the kit from Settings, so none changed. "Adversarial: unlock and quick unlock" ("a quick-unlocked session cannot read the Emergency Kit without the account password"); "Screen models: Emergency Kit and revealed secrets". |
| Z87 | Low | X4's export half. The three plaintext exporters left out every trashed item with "is in the Trash and was left out", including items sync left there (kept after a deletion that had not seen them, trashed without seeing every edit, or over a concurrent restore). A person migrating away read that the trash was theirs and deleted the account, losing an edit the never-lose-data rule had kept. | **Fixed.** `ExportSupport.exports` is the one decision: only `Item.isTrashedByPerson` is left out; the rest is written live with `syncTrashExported`, worded as not the person's discard. "Export reports" ("only the person's own trash is left out…"). |
| Z88 | Medium | Synced scan times turned the device's one prefix cache into a cross-vault oracle. A check answered from the cache costs no pacing token, so the next item is scanned at once, and each check's `scannedAt` is published in the vault's scan records; with one cache for every vault, A4 planted probe items in a shared vault the victim writes to and read from the victim device's times which probes hit a range it had cached for some password in a vault A4 is not in: a confirmation oracle over passwords §2 says A4 must not reach. Not live in shipped builds (neither hygiene nor sync is configured there). | **Fixed** at the root, as the verifier put it: the `PwnedRangeCache` port files every range under the vault whose check fetched it (`HygieneEngine` passes the item's vault), in memory (`PwnedRangeCacheKey`) and in the sealed records' names and index (format 2), so a probe in a shared vault is answered only by ranges fetched for that vault. The same prefix in two vaults costs two requests. Rounding `scannedAt` was not done: on its own it does not close the channel. "Adversarial: hygiene in the vault session" ("a range cached for one vault does not answer a check in another"). |
| Z89 | Low | Each sealed range record's length was 20 bytes plus 22 for every breached suffix in its range, a public property of the prefix, so A2, A3 or A8 listing the device-local store narrowed which prefix a file holds, and the stable name let a watcher follow one file across the weekly rewrites. The verifier judged one listing weak and the trajectory the real leak. | **Fixed.** Every record is padded to a 32 KiB bucket before it is sealed (`CachedPwnedRange.encodedPadded`, read back by `init(decodingPadded:)`, which refuses anything but zeros after the entry). §13.1 says what the files still show: their number and their modification times. "Adversarial: hygiene in the vault session" ("sealed range records have the same length whatever their range holds"). |
| Z90 | Low | The verifier's addition to Z80: on macOS `applicationWillTerminate` cleared the clipboard and did not fire the lock signal, so nothing a screen model drops on a lock (an export's bytes, a merge review, a typed password) was dropped on quit. | **Fixed** (*Mac only*). `AppModel.prepareForTermination` fires the lock signal, hides revealed secrets, sweeps the staging folder and clears the clipboard; the macOS delegate calls it from `applicationWillTerminate`, and iOS from `willTerminateNotification`, which the system posts for a running app, not a suspended one. The session's own lock would finish after the process; the keys go with it. |
| Z95 | Low | F5.7 stopped at entry fields and the app-switcher cover: a revealed password, conflict or merge value, or the Secret Key on the kit was plain text, recorded and mirrored like any other while the app was active, and on macOS stayed on screen while another app was in front; docs/04 had no F5.7 row. The viewer of a shared screen is outside A1 to A8, as the verifier noted. | **Fixed, the cheap half.** `ScreenPrivacy` (portable) hides every revealed value again when the app resigns active and when the screen starts being captured, and refuses to reveal while it is; views keep `RevealedSecrets`, and the kit's model asks the same. iOS reports capture from the scene's `sceneCaptureState`; macOS windows get `sharingType = .none`. PRD F5.7 and docs/04 say what is promised and what is not (a camera; a capture the system does not report). A Debug `--uitest` launch keeps macOS windows capturable and does not hide on resign-active, for the screenshot tour. The hooks are *Mac only*. "Screen models: Emergency Kit and revealed secrets". |
| Z96 | Low | macOS windows were restorable, so a quit or logout with the vault unlocked could have AppKit write a window's state and picture under `Saved Application State`, the one plaintext vault content on disk for A8 (unverified). | **Tracked** (*Mac only*). The first fix, `WindowProtection` marking every window `isRestorable = false`, was reverted: on the CI runner every launch after the first then opened no window at all (run 44273695553: the first macOS UI test passed, the next eight found only the menu bar), because AppKit saved "no restorable windows" at the quit and SwiftUI restored that. The fix that keeps a window at launch is the `WindowGroup`'s `.restorationBehavior(.disabled)` (macOS 15 and later); with a macOS 14 deployment target it needs an availability branch that has not been tried. The device check stays in docs/04 under F5.7. |
| Z97 | Low | The account-password field is tagged `.password` and the item editor pairs a user name with a secure field, which may invite iOS's Password AutoFill and its "Save Password?" prompt into a second store under another threat model (unverified: it depends on what iOS does for an app with no associated domains); the Notes field, where people put recovery phrases, autocorrected and capitalised. | **Partly fixed, partly tracked.** Notes no longer autocorrect or capitalise (*Mac only*). The content type is left as it is: `.oneTimeCode` is a workaround, not a documented opt-out, and needs a device. docs/04 describes the check a person makes (QuickType key, save prompt, then `.oneTimeCode` if either appears). |
| Z98 | Low | The Keychain adapter left unwiped heap copies of the Local Unlock Key, the Secret Key and the device seed: `store` handed `SecItemAdd` an array and a `Data` made from the secret, a read's `CFData` was released unwiped, and the quick-unlock state was decoded through an unwiped `Data`; §11 said `SecItemCopyMatching` returned Keychain data straight into `SecureBytes`. A post-lock memory image could hold the LUK (whether freed blocks survive was not settled). | **Fixed in part, docs corrected, the rest tracked.** `store` makes one `Data` straight from `SecureBytes` and zeroes it once the query is gone (*Mac only*); the quick-unlock state is decoded from one such copy and zeroed. Both need no unsafe construct. Wiping the framework's own `CFData` would need a write through a pointer to storage this code does not own, beyond ADR 0001's bindings: §11 now names that copy and the framework's internal ones as remaining. No adversarial test can observe freed memory; the Mac round trips (`SystemKeychainStoreTests`) cover that the stored value is unchanged. |
| Z99 | Low | Found by the review of this change (Z80). `AppModel.start()` swept the staging folder on every call, and `.task { await model.start() }` sits on the `WindowGroup`'s root view, so it runs once per window; `enterVault()`, reached from `start()` on an unlocked session, swept again. On macOS or iPad, with a "Save File…" sheet up in one window, opening a second window deleted the staged file under it and the save failed. The same loss happens at a lock while the mover is up (the idle auto-lock: no activity is recorded while the system sheet is up), which §14.1 did not say. | **Fixed** as the review proposed, and the lock case written down. `StagingSweeper.launched()` sweeps on its first call only; `start()` calls it, and `enterVault(sweeping: false)` from `start()` does not sweep a session that was already unlocked (an unlock, a lock and termination still sweep; `AppEnvironment.make` sweeps before the session exists). §14.1 says that a lock while the mover is up loses that save. "Screen models: staged files" ("a second window's start does not remove a file another window is saving"; failed with the once-only guard removed, which is the sweep as it was). `Apps/Shared/AppModel.swift` *Mac only*. |
| Z126 | Low | Found by the review of this change (Z98, the shape of Z83). The Keychain write's wipe reached the secret only if the `Data` was its buffer's one reference when `resetBytes` ran: the query boxed it in `Any`, `as CFDictionary` bridged it to an `NSData` sharing its storage, and anything that retained that object past `SecItemAdd` turned the wipe into a copy-on-write of a fresh buffer. A8's post-lock memory image could then hold the Local Unlock Key, the Secret Key or the device seed. The quick-unlock decode in the session has the same dependence. | **Fixed for the write; the decode left, with the reason.** `SecureBytes.withMutableDataCopy` (`SecureMemory`, the module allowed unsafe code: one copy into an `NSMutableData`, zeroed through the object after the body) is what `SystemKeychainStore.store` hands `SecItemAdd`, unbridged, so the zeros reach the one buffer whoever kept it, as `EmergencyKitDocument.write` already does. The quick-unlock decode passes its `Data` to `JSONDecoder` only, which returns before the wipe and keeps nothing, so its sole reference holds; it is left as it is (it is in `VaultSession.swift`, which this pass otherwise left alone). Architecture §11. "Adversarial: crypto core" ("a secret's mutable-data copy is zeroed in the object itself, whoever kept a reference to it", the mechanism; the old wipe's shape is shown beside it). `SystemKeychainStore.swift` *Mac only*; the Mac round trips (`SystemKeychainStoreTests`) cover that the stored value is unchanged. |
| Z127 | Low | Found by the review of this change (Z86). `EmergencyKitModel.open()` set `kit` and `shownAt` after awaiting `emergencyKit(password:)`. If `lock()` ran after that call's last `requireKeys(since:)` and the lock signal delivered `dropOnLock` to the main actor before `open()`'s continuation resumed, the model set the kit on a locked vault: the Secret Key String stayed in a live model. `ExportModel.export()` had the same pattern from before, with a plaintext export's bytes. | **Fixed** as the review proposed. Both models count the locks they hear (`dropOnLock`), read the count before awaiting, and keep nothing from a call a lock overtook: the kit is not set, and an export's bytes are zeroised and dropped (also if the drop comes during the titles' read after them). "Screen models: a lock during the password check" (both cases, with the away-work hook holding the session's password check while the model hears the drop; both failed before: the kit and the bytes were kept). |
| Z128 | Low | Found by the review of this change (Z81, Z82; A7). "Restore into a new account" takes the Secret Key as typed text, so A7 can seal any archive under an account of its own and hand over its Kit, and the code cannot tell it from a genuine restore; yet such an archive got the limits sized for this app's own: 1 GiB of stored drafts (a CSV or 1PUX: 512 MiB), four times the scope derivations (which also feed the import review's comparisons), 1,024 URLs and 16,384 fields an item. The budgets were enforced while drafts were built; what grew was the bound. | **Fixed** with the review's first suggestion. An archive opened with a typed Secret Key is restored under `InterchangeLimits.archiveOfAnotherAccount`: the format's 200,000 items and `archive`'s per-item bounds (1 MiB stored, 16,384 parts, the field size), with `standard`'s import-wide budgets (512 MiB stored, a million fields, 100,000 scope derivations, 32 URLs an item). An archive opened with this device's own Secret Key keeps `archive`. Since a genuine restore into a new account is the archive's main use, `exportArchive`'s restore check now also runs the items under the other account's budgets and says when the archive would not restore into a new account (`ArchiveRestoreCheck.limitReachedInAnotherAccount`, worded on the export screen); it still restores into this one. Architecture §14.1. "Adversarial: interchange through the session" ("an archive of another account gets the import-wide budgets a CSV gets": 700 entries each stored just under the 1 MiB bound, some 570 MiB; failed before: restored; the export check case is in the same test). |
| Z129 | Low | Found by the review of this change (Z80; A8, or anyone who writes the temporary directory of an unsandboxed build). `stage` created its sub-folder with intermediate directories under `<tmp>/Watchword Staging`, a path it trusted: made a symbolic link, it had the plaintext written through it. In the sandboxed app the target must be in the container; in ad-hoc-signed unsandboxed builds (`app:test macos`, CI) it can be any folder the user can write, and `temporaryDirectory` is the per-user `$TMPDIR`, where the sweep of earlier builds' `Export-*` folders also removed any other program's folder of that name. | **Fixed** as the review proposed. Before writing, `stage` reads what is at the folder's path without following a link; anything but a real directory is removed, the folder is made again without intermediates, and the per-file sub-folder is made without them too. The earlier-build sweep removes only `Export-` followed by a UUID, a real directory holding one regular file. A swap between the check and the write is not excluded. Architecture §14.1. "Screen models: staged files" ("a staging folder replaced by a link is not written through, and the earlier sweep removes only its own"; failed before: the file was written through the link and both foreign folders removed). |
| Z130 | Low | Found by the review of this change (Z95). On iOS capture was registered once, for the scenes connected at the first `startMonitoring`, and kept as one flag: an iPad window opened later was never registered, a registered scene that closed left `isCaptured` at its last value, and with two scenes the one not captured reporting "not captured" cleared the flag the captured one had set, so a secret could be revealed while a window was being recorded. | **Fixed** with the decision in the portable model. `ScreenPrivacy` keeps the scenes reported captured (`captureChanged(_:in:)`, `sceneDisconnected`) and counts the screen captured while any connected one is; the one-scene `captureChanged(_:)` stays for a single-window app. The iOS shell registers every scene connected at start and each that connects later (`UIScene.willConnectNotification`), and drops one that disconnects. "Screen models: Emergency Kit and revealed secrets" ("the screen counts as captured while any connected scene is"; the sequence that failed, a second scene's "not captured" clearing the first's capture, cannot be expressed in the old one-flag API, which is the defect). `Apps/iOS/PlatformLifecycle.swift` *Mac only*, and whether `willConnectNotification` fires before the first trait report on a device is unverified. |
| Z131 | Low (functional) | Found by the review of this change (Z86, F5.1). Since Z86, `accountPasswordRequired` from `unconfirmedEmergencyKit()` opens the vault after a quick unlock, and the kit opened from Settings (`isOnboarding == false`) showed only "Done"; nothing else called `confirmEmergencyKitSaved`, so a person who only quick-unlocks stayed unconfirmed until their next password unlock. | **Fixed.** `EmergencyKitModel` reads, when the kit opens from Settings, whether the account records a confirmation (`awaitsConfirmation`) and confirms it (`confirmSaved()`); the Settings kit shows the same toggle and a Confirm button while it is unconfirmed (new identifiers `kit.settingsConfirmSaved`, `kit.settingsConfirm`; the onboarding ones are unchanged). "Screen models: Emergency Kit and revealed secrets" ("the kit opened from Settings can be confirmed while the account records no confirmation"; the model had no way to confirm before, which is the defect). `Apps/Shared/EmergencyKitView.swift` *Mac only*; no UI test drives it. |
| Z132 | Info | Found by the review of this change (Z85). "Every date" did not cover a passkey's creation date, which lives inside its field's value (CXF's import, an archive's verbatim field): A7's could stay in the future. Nothing security-relevant reads it today. Separately, `boundingDates` was a linear pass over up to 200,000 drafts on the session's actor, between the epoch checks in `stage`, where CLAUDE.md puts such steps off the actor. | **Fixed, both.** `boundingDates` bounds each decodable passkey's `createdAt`, in the fields and in every history entry, re-encoding the field and reporting `futureDateBounded`; and `stage` runs it off the actor (`AwayWork.importDates`), so a lock during it wins. "Adversarial: interchange through the session" ("a file cannot date a passkey in the future either", failed with the passkey bound removed; "a lock while an import's dates are bounded takes effect at once", which with the step on the actor never reaches the hook, and hung so when tried). |
| Z133 | Info | Found by the review of this change: two slips in its tests. The Z88 test in `AdversarialSessionHygieneTests` was inserted between `plantedRecordsAreIgnored`'s A8 doc comment and its function, so `cachePartitionedByVault` carried two threat statements and `plantedRecordsAreIgnored` none, which CLAUDE.md requires of every adversarial test. And `StagingTests.launchSweep`'s message "an earlier build's leftover stays" contradicted its assertion, which checks that the leftover is removed. | **Fixed.** The A8 comment is back on `plantedRecordsAreIgnored`; the message reads "an earlier build's leftover is removed" (changed with Z99). No behaviour changed. |
| Z134 | Info | Found by the review of this change, unverified points. (a) `sharingType = .none` and ScreenCaptureKit: macOS 15 and later are reported to capture such windows anyway, while PRD F5.7 and `ScreenPrivacy`'s documentation promised a blank shared screen. (b) The kit's mover-dismissal handler (one `Task.yield()`, then remove), copied from `ExportView`: if SwiftUI clears `isPresented` before the move completes, it deletes the file under the mover. (c) `EmergencyKitDocument.render` grew its `NSMutableData` by reallocation, so earlier fragments of the PDF, the Secret Key's text and QR among them, were freed unwiped. | **(a) Docs corrected:** PRD F5.7 and `ScreenPrivacy` now say the windows ask to be left out of capture and that a blank shared screen is not promised until docs/04's manual check passes on a current macOS (docs/04 already holds the check; it is unchanged here). **(b) Tracked:** neither ordering has been seen; the handler is unchanged in both views, and the check belongs to the same Mac run (save the kit and an export to a slow provider, and dismiss the mover by swiping). **(c) Fixed** (*Mac only*): the buffer is given 1 MiB of capacity up front (`renderCapacity`), far above a one-page kit, so it does not grow while drawing; that `NSMutableData(capacity:)` allocates up front, rather than taking a hint, is the platform's behaviour and unverified here. |

What this part of the audit did not settle. **Decisions that are the owner's.** Whether to try `.oneTimeCode` on the
account-password and item-password fields once a device shows what iOS offers (Z97); whether the remaining
Keychain `CFData` copy (Z98) warrants a SecureMemory binding that reads into protected storage directly, if the
Security framework offers one; the size of the cache's padding bucket and the 512 MiB import budget, both judgements
(Z81, Z89). **The Mac.** Everything here was built and run on Linux. `Apps/Shared/AppEnvironment.swift`,
`AppModel.swift`, `ConflictView.swift`, `EmergencyKitView.swift`, `ExportView.swift`, `ImportView.swift`,
`ItemDetailView.swift`, `ItemEditView.swift`, `RootView.swift` and `SettingsView.swift`, `Apps/iOS/PlatformLifecycle.swift`
and `Apps/macOS/PlatformLifecycle.swift`, and `WatchwordPlatform`'s `EmergencyKitDocument.swift` and
`SystemKeychainStore.swift` changed and have not been compiled; the termination sweep, the capture hiding, the
window settings and the kit's file mover have not run anywhere. The review's rows changed `Apps/Shared/AppModel.swift`
(Z99), `Apps/Shared/EmergencyKitView.swift` (Z131), `Apps/iOS/PlatformLifecycle.swift` (Z130), and
`SystemKeychainStore.swift` (Z126) and `EmergencyKitDocument.swift` (Z134) again: not compiled either. **Decisions
that are the owner's, from the review:** whether an archive that is too large to restore into a new account under a
file's budgets (Z128) should instead be refused when it is made, and what the mover's dismissal should do if (b) of
Z134 is seen.

### CI, supply chain and documentation claims (Z100 to Z119)

The adversaries are those of `07-supply-chain.md` §1 (**T1** a dependency's author, **T2** an action's, **T3** a build
tool's, **T5** whoever can push a branch or open a pull request) and, for the last two rows, **A2** and the breach-check
service of architecture §2 and §13.1. Z102 to Z106 were reproduced by script tests (`Tests/ScriptTests`, `mise run
test:scripts`) that ran the scripts as they were and failed; Z100 and Z101 concern a workflow and repository settings
that nothing in this repository can run or read, and Z107 and Z108 are claims in the documents. *Mac only* marks what
compiles or runs only with Xcode and has not run yet.

| ID | Severity | Finding and threat | Disposition |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Z100 | Medium | `claude-pr.yml`'s stated bound was wrong. `07-supply-chain.md` §3.2 named prompt injection as the residual and said the tool allowlist "bounds what it can do to commenting on that one pull request". Commenting is the disclosure channel: the action runs Claude Code with the job's secrets in its process environment (the Anthropic key or OAuth token and the job's `pull-requests: write` token; read in `action.yml` at the pinned commit), `Read` has no path limit, `Bash(gh pr comment <n>:*)` is a prefix rule that admits `--body-file`, and the thread reply's `gh api` rule admits `--input` and `-F body=@file`. On `issue_comment` the default branch's copy runs with the secret on a fork's pull request by design, so the author of a fork pull request (T5, with no access to the repository) whose text steers a review an admin asked for could have the secrets posted in a public comment. | **Tracked; documents corrected.** §3.2 and the workflow's header now state the bound as it is (what Claude can change, not what a comment can say), what a steered review could post, and the three changes that would narrow it: the subprocess environment scrub the pinned action passes through, deny rules beside the allowlist, and refusing fork pull requests on `issue_comment`. None is applied. Whether `gh` keeps its token under the scrub, and whether a deny rule on a command's arguments holds for the CLI the action installs, cannot be established without running the workflow, and a wrong guess breaks every review with no red check. The test to run first is in §3.2 (a scratch repository, a dummy secret, the admin asking for each command outright). Not reproduced: needs that run. |
| Z101 | Low | "Admin only" was claimed of `claude-pr.yml` as a whole and holds for one trigger. On `pull_request`, `pull_request_review` and `pull_request_review_comment` GitHub runs the pull request's own copy of the workflow, in which the `authorize` job is whatever the branch says, and any workflow on a same-repository branch can name `environment: staging`. For the likeliest holder of write access, a prompt-influenced session or a stolen token acting as the owner, `authorize` passes unedited on every trigger. What keeps the secret is the environment's required reviewer and deployment-branch rule, which no runbook created or verified. | **Documents corrected; settings for the owner.** §3.2, `CLAUDE.md` and the workflow's comments say which control binds where. `08-repository-hardening.md` §1.2 creates `staging` with a required reviewer and the two deployment refs, keeps the secret out of repository secrets, and says what to read before approving a run; its §9 prints the reviewers, the policies and both lists of secret names. Whether the environment is protected today cannot be read from the tree: until §1.2 has been run and §9 checked, it is unverified. |
| Z102 | Medium | The swift-crypto pin did not reach the apps. ADR 0006 and `07-supply-chain.md` §2 marked as enforced that the dependency is built at two named commits, because the root `Package.resolved` is committed and compared. That file governs `swift build` and `swift test`. The apps are built by xcodebuild from a generated project that names the package by path; Xcode resolves a local package's dependencies itself, by the `4.5.2` tag and the newest swift-asn1 in range. Nothing checked what it fetched, so a re-pointed tag (T1) left every package gate green and was compiled into the next `app:build` or `app:test`, with the vault's keys in reach at run time. | **Fixed; *Mac only*, and unproven until a Mac run passes.** The first fix read the `Package.resolved` Xcode was expected to write inside the generated project. The first Mac run (Xcode 27) showed that it writes none there for this project, a local package whose dependencies are remote: `-resolvePackageDependencies` succeeded without a scheme, the packages resolved, and the file did not exist, so every app job failed closed. The evidence is now the sources that are compiled. Every `xcodebuild` call in `app:build`, `app:test`, `app:release` and `app:test-keychain`, and the one in `pick-simulator.sh`, is given one `-clonedSourcePackagesDirPath` (`.build/app-source-packages`, `mise-tasks/app/packages.sh`), so each dependency is a git working copy at a place the task chose. The tasks have Xcode resolve first and stop before compiling unless `assert-no-dependencies.sh --checkouts` finds under `checkouts/` exactly the allowed packages, each a working copy of its own with `HEAD` at the commit the script names and no file changed, and they check again after the build; no checkouts is a failure, and git is not let to look above the directory for a repository. A failure prints the listings, every checkout's `HEAD` and what Xcode recorded in `workspace-state.json`, so one run says what is there. ADR 0006 is amended: the pin covers an app only through that step. `test_app_tasks.py`, with a stand-in `xcodebuild` that checks packages out with real git: "nothing is built from any other commit", "no checkouts at all is a failure", "a checkout that was changed is refused", "a directory that is no working copy is refused", "a build that resolved again is caught afterwards", "a resolution that was not given the directory proves nothing", and two that fail if any `xcodebuild` call lacks the argument. Still from Xcode's documented behaviour and not seen: that it puts the working copies there and leaves them clean. A call without the argument would compile from Xcode's own DerivedData, which nothing reads; the tests are what hold that. One consequence of letting Xcode resolve and then judging the result: on a fresh runner it takes the newest swift-asn1 in swift-crypto's range, so the day upstream releases one, `app-tests` and `release-build` turn red on every pull request until `ALLOWED_PINS` and the root `Package.resolved` are bumped. That is the check failing closed on a day upstream chooses, and it is the expected trigger for the bump, not a fault to work round; handing Xcode the committed pins, so that it resolves to them instead, is to be tried once a Mac run shows what it accepts. |
| Z103 | Low | The `Apps/project.yml` tripwire that G's C1 records as fixed was four line `grep`s, and some eighty planted cases passed them: shell at generation time (`options.postGenCommand`) and at build time (scheme actions, a `legacy` or aggregate target, `buildScripts`), files no check reads (`include`, `configFiles`), a source path outside `Apps/` (native code compiled into the app and read by neither tripwire), a remote package in flow style, quoted and escaped keys, a `framework:` dependency written as a list entry, `SWIFT_STRICT_MEMORY_SAFETY: "NO"` in quotes, and the same line simply deleted, after which nothing checks the app and extension code for unmarked unsafe constructs. Each needs a merged pull request (T5); the shell cases add no capability a pull request lacks. | **Fixed.** The spec is parsed (`.github/scripts/yaml-audit.py`) and held to an allowlist of top-level keys, option keys, target types, per-target keys, dependency kinds, scheme keys and build settings; the one package is the repository's own by path; source paths stay inside `Apps/` but for the one named test file; the settings the app code is checked under must be present with their values; no per-configuration settings; no `${…}` taken from the environment. The reader takes the subset of YAML the spec is written in and refuses the rest. C1's entry is annotated. `test_dependency_tripwire.py` (each planted case, through the script itself). Left: the generated project is not inspected, and `info` and `entitlements` properties are not policed. |
| Z104 | Low | Three gates could be satisfied while violating what they guard. `audit:actions` was a `grep` for one spelling of `uses:`: a quoted or escaped key, a flow mapping, a space before the colon and a value on the next line all passed, a pinned value in quotes failed, and the word in a script was taken for an action; it ran nightly only, and `audit:mise-pins` in no workflow. The adversarial-suite check was a substring search of the log, so "sync log" was satisfied by "sync log, item metadata", by a test's title, and by the line reporting a suite skipped. The manifest checks read `swift package dump-package`, which is what `Package.swift` evaluates to on the machine running them, so a manifest that reads its environment shows CI a clean dump; and a Swift 5 language mode was not refused. | **Fixed.** `audit:actions` reads every workflow, and any composite action under `.github/`, with the same reader and refuses what it cannot read; both audits are steps of `ci.yml`'s `lint` job, which `Required checks` needs. The suite check needs Swift Testing's own `Suite "Adversarial: <area>" passed after` line with nothing but the result symbol before it. A manifest may import only `PackageDescription` and may not name `#if`, `Context`, `ProcessInfo`, `CommandLine`, `getenv`, `#file` or `random` (refused as words, so an alias is refused where it is made); the package must be in Swift 6 mode with no target put back. A list of words is a tripwire and cannot be complete: Z111 widened it and says what would replace it. `test_audit_actions.py` (15 spellings, line separators, reusable workflows), `test_audit_tasks.py`, `test_dependency_tripwire.py` "a manifest that reads its environment is refused". Where PyYAML is present, `test_yaml_reader.py` compares the reader with it on every audited file. Residual: the reader is this repository's code; it refuses rather than interprets for that reason. Whether Swift Testing names a disabled suite on a "passed" line was not run; the check does not depend on it. |
| Z105 | Low | No job built the Release configuration. Every Debug-only facility (the `--uitest` switch with its in-memory store and Keychain, KDF floor and no auto-lock, `UITestPeer`, the stand-in file sources) is behind `#if DEBUG`, but every scheme and CI leg builds Debug, so that they are absent from a shipped build was asserted by comments, and a Release-only compile error would be found by whoever shipped. No attack today; a spec edit or an XcodeGen default that defined `DEBUG` for Release would have shipped the switch with every check green. | **Fixed; *Mac only*.** `mise run app:release` builds both apps in Release and fails if the app bundle or its embedded AutoFill extension contains `--uitest`, `WATCHWORD_UITEST_PASSWORD`, `UI test: another device` or `Lock now (UI test)`; each marker must still be in the sources, so a rename cannot turn the search into a pass. `ci.yml`'s `release-build` job runs it for macOS and the iOS simulator and `Required checks` needs it; `ci:gate` and `mac-run.yml` take it as a gate. Z103 already refuses a `DEBUG` condition or any per-configuration setting in the spec. `test_app_tasks.py` "a debug-only string in the app fails", "the markers are compiled for debug only". Limits: a literal of 15 bytes or fewer can be built in registers and need not appear as bytes in an arm64 binary, so the long markers are the evidence (`--uitest-appearance=` among them); and the first Mac run may fail on a Release-only build error, which would be a real one. |
| Z106 | Low | `cloud-setup.sh`, which runs as root on the VM holding a cloud session's checkout, verified the Swift tarball against a pinned key and answered "SIGNATURE VERIFICATION FAILED" by running swiftly, downloaded from an unversioned URL with no checksum or signature.
